3.01   Purpose
The purpose of Falcon Hill is to attract a diversity of businesses connected to
aerospace and defense activities. Falcon Hill allows for aerospace and research
industries and necessary services for tenants and employees. Land uses with any
significant adverse impacts (such as excessive noise levels, pollutants,
hazards or nuisances) shall be strictly prohibited. These Development Standards
will be administered to foster high-quality development.
3.02   Site Plan Approval
Site Plan approval is required for all development in Falcon Hill. The review
shall include, without limitation, architectural design and theme, building
materials, lighting, signage, landscaping, parking, vehicular, bike and
pedestrian access, and accessory structures.
3.03   Permitted Uses
Permitted uses in Falcon Hill include:
   A.   General office buildings;
   B.   Any research, educational or public service function of HAFB;
   C.   Laboratories, offices, and prototype production facilities, or research
related assembly of equipment or components, which are related to the onsite
research and development activities of the tenant.
   D.   Light Manufacturing;
   E.   Warehousing and storage incidental to and in support of the above;
   F.   Retail uses incidental to and in support of the above, such as hotels,
meeting facilities, cafeterias, restaurants and other food services, child
care, recreation facilities, stores, and such service facilities as banking and
postal services;
   G.   Parking, parking structures, and driveways incidental to building use;
and
   H.   Wall, Monument, Wayfinding, Awnings, and Pylon Signs (refer to section
5.02).
3.04   Conditional Uses
Conditional uses in Falcon Hill include:
   A.   Any special uses customarily incidental to the operation of other
permitted uses;
   B.   Any uses, not otherwise prohibited, which are supportive of the mission
of HAFB;
   C.   Quarters for a caretaker or for security;
   D.   Radio and/or television tower;
   E.   Temporary structures;
   F.   Wireless telecommunication towers;
   G.   Outdoor Storage (to be adequately screened);
   H.   Support and maintenance shops for the above;
   I.   Freeway Oriented Project Signs.
3.05   Prohibited Uses
Prohibited uses in Falcon Hill include:
   A.   Ultra hazardous activities involving the storage, treatment,
transportation, disposal, or manufacture of hazardous materials, hazardous
substances, or hazardous wastes, except as specifically authorized by HAFB;
   B.   Correction or detention facilities;
   C.   Arms or munitions testing;
   D.   Timber cutting, mining operations, sand or gravel removal;
   E.   Residential uses;
   F.   Big Box Retail uses.
3.06   Use Exceptions
The Executive Director may determine if a use not listed here is consistent
with and should be listed as a permitted use, a conditional use, or a
prohibited use. If the Executive Director determines that a use should be a
permitted use or a conditional use, that determination shall be communicated to
the DRC and the MIDA Board. The Applicant may continue with the Site Plan
Review unless the MIDA Board makes a decision to overrule the Executive
Director’s determination before the Site Plan is approved. If the Executive
Director determines that a use is a prohibited use that decision is immediately
appealable to the MIDA Board at its next regularly scheduled board meeting.
3.07   Variations
Any request for variation or exception from the Development Standards shall be
submitted in written form as a conditional use, and may be approved or
considered by MIDA without having to amend or revise the standards herein.
3.08   Master Development Concept Plan Required
Any development in Falcon Hill shall be subject to the MDCP approved by MIDA.
3.09   Setbacks/Yard Requirements
All setbacks/yard requirements will provide sufficient space required for fire
protection and shall promote and protect the health, safety and welfare of
those in Falcon Hill and the surrounding areas.
3.10   Buildings/Improvements
   A.   Any building erected or improvements on the premises must be designed
by a licensed architect/engineer as required by State law and conform to all
applicable codes.
   B.   Any development erected according to the DBBF is subject to the Air
Force codes, regulations, and inspections.
   C.   All buildings must follow the architectural design guidelines found in
Appendix A, Chapter 6.
3.11   Maximum Height of Structures
The maximum height of structures in Falcon Hill is ten (10) stories. MIDA may
allow additional height upon finding that the additional height will not
adversely affect the surrounding land uses. MIDA may also reduce the height
upon finding that such reduction is necessary to reduce the adverse impact to
surrounding land uses or otherwise is necessary to protect public health,
safety, or welfare. Any building over four (4) stories must have the approval
of HAFB.
3.12   Landscaping Requirement
   A.   Landscaping:
      1.   The appearance of yard and grounds is critical in achieving the
atmosphere desired in Falcon Hill. Careful attention should be given to this
element of the site plan. As a minimum, the following shall be required of all
users of the land:
         a.   Design and coordination of a landscape plan by a licensed
landscape architect.
         b.   Not less than twenty percent (20%) of land area in landscape,
decorative hardscape, and planting within all approved Site Plans. The
landscaping percentage may be lowered by the MIDA Board if a landscape offset
plan is approved.
         c.   Planting as a part of the initial construction contract.
         d.   Adequate irrigation or sprinkling of all planted areas.
         e.   Clean, neat and prompt maintenance of all premises.
         f.   Adequate screening of parking by planting, berming and/or
fencing.
         g.   All fencing must be of permanent material, and be well
maintained.
         h.   All landscaping must follow the landscaping design guidelines
found in Appendix A, Chapter 6.
   B.   Air Force Security Review of Landscaping
      1.   If an Air Force security review of a plan indicates that a
modification is needed to the landscaping requirements of this chapter for a
particular site to maintain security, then the modification shall be allowed.
3.13   Lighting
   A.   Uniformity of lighting is desirable to achieve an overall objective of
continuity, and to avoid objectionable glare.
   B.   The maximum height of luminaries shall be thirty (30) feet unless the
MIDA Board requires a lower height as part of its approvals. The light shall be
low intensity, shielded from uses on adjoining lots, and directed away from
adjacent properties.
   C.   All parking luminaries, except those required for security, shall be
extinguished one hour after the end of business hours. The exception for
security lighting applies to a maximum of twenty-five percent (25%) of the
total luminaries used, unless the MIDA Board approves a higher percentage.
   D.   Pedestrian walkways to mass transit facilities shall be lighted.
   E.   All lighting shall be full-cut-off lighting to reduce light pollution.
   F.   Lighting on signage – see Appendix A, Chapter 5 “Signage”.
   G.   All lighting must follow the lighting design guidelines found in
Appendix A, Chapter 6.
3.14   Nuisance Factors and Hazards
   A.   Activities that emit offensive or objectionable noise, vibration,
smoke, odors, dust or gases, air pollution, or water pollution or generate
heavy truck traffic are not permitted. Precautions shall be taken in all
operations against radiation, radioactivity, fire and explosion hazards.
   B.   Activities conducted on the premises shall comply with all state and
federal laws, regulations and permits.
   C.   The noise level emanating from any use or operation shall not exceed
the limits in the health department standards adopted by MIDA regarding noise
control.
   D.   A use shall not be permitted which creates objectionable odor in such
quantity as to be readily detectable at the boundaries of the building site.
3.15   Screening
   A.   All trash or refuse receptacle areas shall be completely screened from
public view or shall be enclosed within a building.
   B.   All ground mounted mechanical equipment, including, without limitation,
heating and air conditioning units shall be completely screened from public
view or shall be enclosed within a building.
   C.   The use of roof appurtenances is discouraged. If roof appurtenances
(including, but not limited to, air conditioning units and mechanical
equipment) are used, they shall be placed within an enclosure (at least as high
as the roof appurtenances) that reflects the architectural design scheme of the
project or provide a site line study that show the appurtenances are hidden
from public view. Such enclosures require the MIDA Board’s approval and shall
minimize visibility from on-site parking areas, adjacent public streets, and
adjacent property.
   D.   All utility equipment shall be compatible with the architectural
elements of the site and not be exposed except where necessary for reasonable
access or where required by utility company. Pad-mounted transformers and/or
meter box locations shall be included in the Site Plan with an appropriate
screening treatment. Power lines and other utility cables shall be installed
underground where possible.
   E.   Loading areas and docks shall be screened by landscaping and/or visual
barriers from adjacent properties and public streets.
   F.   All screening must follow the design guidelines found in Appendix A,
Chapter 6.
3.16   Access and Parking.
   A.   The number of access points along the streets shall be minimized by
sharing and linking parking areas with adjacent properties. Reciprocal ingress
and egress, circulation and parking agreements controlled or managed by Sunset
Ridge shall be required to facilitate the ease of vehicular movement between
adjoining properties. On corner sites, access points shall be located as far
from the corner as reasonably possible and in no case less than sixty (60) feet
from the point of intersection of the property lines.
   B.   Parking shall be located peripherally around the buildings rather than
concentrated between the building and the public streets to allow the building
to be closer to one another and to mass transit facilities.
   C.   Parking spaces for vanpool/carpool vehicles shall be provided and have
a priority location near building entrances to encourage this form of mass
transit.
   D.   All developments in Falcon Hill will be required to provide parking on-
site. No on-street parking will count towards the parking calculation, unless
allowed with MIDA approval. All developments shall feature landscape islands in
parking areas compliant with Appendix A, Chapter 4, “Off-Street Parking
Requirements.”
3.17   Pedestrian Walkways
Pedestrian walkways, a minimum of five (5) feet wide, shall be provided to
accommodate pedestrian movement between activity centers within the site to
adjacent uses and from building entrances directly to mass transit facilities.
3.18   General Requirements
All building and on-site signage must comply with Appendix A, Chapter 5,
“Signage.”
3.19   Financial Responsibility
The Applicant shall post a performance and payment bond, other security, or
proof of financial responsibility for all On-site and Off-site Improvements, as
required by MIDA.
3.20   Enforcement
These Development Standards may be enforced by MIDA. Violation of any standard
herein contained shall give MIDA the right to bring an action against the party
or parties violating or attempting to violate any of the Development Standards,
to enjoin them from so doing, to cause any such violation to be remedied, or to
recover damages resulting from such violation, including attorney’s fees.
CHAPTER 4 OFF STREET PARKING REQUIREMENTS
4.01   Purpose
4.02   Required Parking
4.03   General Provisions
4.04   Submittal and Approval of Parking Areas
4.05   Assessing Parking Requirements
4.06   Dimensions for Parking Stalls
4.07   Handicapped Accessible Parking
4.08   Landscaping in Parking Areas
4.09   Pedestrian Walkways and Accesses
4.10   Shared Parking and Curb Cuts
4.11   Required Parking by Land Use
4.12   Charts and Graphs
4.01   Purpose
The purpose of this Chapter is to reduce congestion and traffic hazards on
public rights of way by requiring adequate, functional, and effective use of
off-street parking areas. This Chapter also establishes minimum landscaping
requirements in order to reduce adverse impacts of headlight glare and lighting
within the parking area; improve circulation within parking areas by channeling
vehicles and pedestrians; provide climatic relief from broad expanses of
pavement; and improve the appearance of the site and surrounding areas.
4.02   Required Parking
Off-street parking shall be provided according to standards noted in this
Chapter for all newly constructed buildings and additional parking shall be
provided for any structure or use that is expanded.
4.03   General Provisions
   A.   Materials for Parking Areas: Parking areas shall consist of concrete,
asphalt, or other permanent materials approved in the MIDA’s adopted
construction standards.
   B.   Maintenance of Parking Areas: Pavement, striping, landscaping, and
lighting are required to be maintained in all parking areas. During times of
snowfall, parking areas shall be cleared of snow as soon as is practically
possible and otherwise in accordance with this Code.
   C.   Parking Area Access: Parking areas for one (1) or more structures may
have a common access. The determination of the locations for a common access
shall be based upon the geometry, road alignment, and traffic volumes of the
accessed road. All structures are required to provide parking areas where
automobiles will not back across a sidewalk to gain access onto a street.
   D.   Lighting in Parking Areas: Parking areas shall have adequate lighting
to insure the safe circulation of automobiles and pedestrians. Such lighting
shall be shielded in such a way as to not be a nuisance to, or otherwise
adversely affect, adjacent properties or uses.
   E.   Location of Parking Areas: Required off-street parking areas shall be
placed within six hundred (600) feet of the main entrance to the building. This
requirement may be waived by MIDA.
   F.   Stormwater Runoff: All parking areas shall be reviewed and approved by
the MIDA engineer for adequate drainage of stormwater runoff.
4.04   Submittal and Approval of Parking Areas
Plans depicting the parking areas for newly constructed buildings and expanded
structures or uses shall be submitted in conjunction with a Site Plan for all
development. All parking plans shall show the following: the required number of
stalls and aisles scaled to the correct dimensions, the correct number of
handicapped accessible parking spaces, stormwater drainage capabilities,
lighting, landscaping, areas to be irrigated, and pedestrian walkways.
4.05   Assessing Parking Requirements
The following criteria shall be used in conjunction with the standards of the
Institute of Transportation Engineers (ITE) Summary of Parking Generation Rates
referred to in Section
4.12 when determining required parking for any project:
   A.   When a parking requirement is based upon square footage, the assessed
parking shall be based upon net square footage of the building or use.
   B.   When parking requirements are based upon the number of employees,
parking calculations shall use the largest number of employees who work at any
one shift. Where shifts changes may cause substantial over-crowding of parking
facilities, additional stalls may be required.
   C.   When a development contains multiple uses, more than one parking
requirement may be applied.
   D.   Where no comparative land use standard for parking is found in the
publication referenced in Section
4.12, MIDA shall determine an appropriate requirement.
   E.   Any information provided by the Applicant relative to trip generation,
hours or operation, shared parking, peak demands, parking management plans,
employee incentives, designated carpooling spaces, or other information
relative to parking shall be considered when evaluating parking needs.
   F.   Parking requirements may deviate from the standards contained in
Section
4.12 when MIDA determines that the deviation meets the intent of this Chapter.
4.06   Dimensions for Parking Stalls
The dimensions of parking stalls and aisles contained within the parking areas
shall be dependent upon the orientation of stalls. The table titled “Dimensions
for Parking Stalls and Aisles” under Section
4.12 details these standards and any deviation to these standards must be
recommended by the MIDA engineer and approved by the MIDA Board. Considerations
will be given to areas specifically designed for hybrid or other energy
efficient vehicle spaces.
4.07   Handicapped Accessible Parking
   A.   Handicapped parking stalls shall be provided in off-street parking
areas and shall count towards fulfilling the minimum requirements for
automobile parking.
   B.   Handicapped parking stalls shall be located as near as practical to a
primary building entrance with access ramps negotiable for equipment used in
assisting handicapped persons. A permanently affixed reflective sign and/or
surface identification painting depicting the standard symbol for handicapped
parking shall identify each parking stall.
   C.   The number of handicapped parking stalls shall conform to the minimum
requirements of the Americans with Disabilities Act (ADA) and the table
detailed in Section
4.12.
   D.   The dimensions of handicapped parking stalls shall adhere to current
ADA parking standards.
4.08   Landscaping in Parking Areas
The following requirements shall apply to all landscaping of off-street parking
areas:
   A.   Parking Areas Adjacent to Streets: All parking areas which are adjacent
to streets shall create a landscaped strip of not less than ten (10) feet in
width placed between the sidewalk and the parking area.
   B.   Curbs: All landscaped areas abutting any paved surface shall be curbed.
Boundary landscaping around the perimeter of the parking areas shall be
separated by a concrete curb six (6) inches higher than the parking surface.
   C.   Clear Sight: At intersections of streets, driveways, and sidewalks all
landscaping shall be limited to a height of not more than three (3) feet. The
grade at such intersections shall not be bermed, or raised for a distance of
thirty (30) feet at intersections and fifteen (15) feet back from driveways to
allow for sight distance.
   D.   Required Parking Islands:
      1.   Islands on Doubled Rows of Parking. On doubled rows of parking
stalls, there shall be one (1) landscaped island measuring 36' x 9' on each end
of the parking rows, plus one (1) landscaped island measuring 36' x 9' placed
at minimum of every forty (40) parking stalls. Each island on doubled parking
rows shall include a minimum of two (2) trees per planter. Continuous
landscaped medians are allowed.
      2.   Islands on Single Rows of Parking: On single rows of parking or
where parking abuts a sidewalk, there shall be one (1) landscaped island,
measuring 18' x 9', a minimum of every twenty (20) stalls. Islands on a single
parking row shall have a minimum of one (1) tree planter.
      3.   Landscaped islands at the ends of parking rows shall be placed and
shaped in such a manner as to help direct traffic through the parking area.
There shall be a break in parking rows at a minimum of eighty (80) parking
stalls for each double row of parking for the purpose of facilitating traffic
circulation on the site.
   E.   Parking Field Area Landscaping Ratios Including Shoulder Areas:
 
Less than 12,000 sq. ft.  0%
12,000 to 29,999 sq. ft.  5%
30,000 sq. ft. and larger 7.5%
 
      1.   One tree adjacent to the on-grade hardscape parking area shall be
planted for every fifteen (15) parking stalls. Deciduous trees shall have a
minimum caliper of one and a half (1½) inches. A minimum of forty percent (40%)
of the trees shall be conifer trees having a minimum height of six (6) feet.
The distribution of the trees shall maximize shading during summer months. All
landscaped areas shall be separated from the parking surface by at least a six
(6) inch high curb.
      2.   Landscaping for parking structures shall meet the following minimum
requirements:
         a.   The parking structures must be screened with live plant material
reasonably acceptable to the Executive Director that is intended to visually
break up the façade and the sides of the structures from public view.
         b.   The landscaping requirement surrounding parking structures shall
be twenty percent (20%) of the parking area footprint. However, the MIDA Board
may require additional landscaping after taking into consideration the number
and size of the parking structures. The landscaping requirement may be lowered
by the MIDA Board, if a landscape offset plan is approved.
   F.   Landscaped Boundary Strips: All landscaped boundary strips shall be a
minimum of five (5) feet in width. A landscaped screen, berming, or fence may
be required by MIDA around the perimeter of the parking area to mitigate
intrusion of lighting from headlights and other lighting on surrounding
property.
   G.   Completion of Landscaping: All landscaping improvements shall be
completed in accordance with the approved Site Plan, landscape-planting plan,
and irrigation plan and shall occur prior to the issuance of a certificate of
occupancy for the building. Exceptions may be permitted, and certificates of
occupancy issued where weather conditions prohibit the completion of required
landscaping improvements. In such cases, an extension period of up to six (6)
months is permitted upon posting of a bond for not less than one hundred ten
percent (110%) of the value of the landscaping, which shall be held until the
requirements of this chapter are met.
   H.   Maintenance. As an ongoing condition of approval, the applicant has an
obligation to maintain the landscaping at a level that is acceptable to MIDA.
   I.   Snow Stacking Capacity. Every parking lot design shall plan for a snow
stacking area to accommodate the stacking volume of a one (1) foot snow base
over the entire parking lot.
4.09   Pedestrian Walkways and Accesses
Pedestrian walkways shall be provided between building entrances or other
concentrated pedestrian nodes where any area where pedestrian traffic will be
obvious and shall extend to the entrance of the building. Landscaped islands
along the walkway shall be provided in continuous strips or placed at a minimum
interval of every thirty (30) feet. Landscaped islands are encouraged to be
offset from one another to create a feeling of greater coverage. Where the
Applicant desires to have a driveway access at the center of the parking area,
a pedestrian access shall be placed on either side of the driveway access.
4.10   Shared Parking and Curb Cuts
Required parking may be shared with the other required parking areas upon
approval by the MIDA Board. The Applicant must show:
   A.   An agreement granting shared parking or mutual access to the entire
parking lot. No agreement is necessary where multiple buildings on site are
controlled or managed by Sunset Ridge.
   B.   A professional traffic engineer shall provide peak demand data showing
that shared parking is feasible. In most cases, shared parking areas shall also
share ingress and egress. This requirement may be waived when the MIDA engineer
believes that shared accesses are not feasible. In reviewing the Site Plan, the
MIDA engineer shall evaluate the need for limited access, appropriate number of
curb cuts, shared driveways or other facilities that will result in a safer and
more efficient parking and circulation pattern.
4.11   Required Parking by Land Use
MIDA adopts the ITE manual of parking generation rates. The MIDA requirement
shall be the average rate of parking for the most intense parking period listed
in the most current edition of such publication for each development. Upon
approval of the MIDA Board, the parking may be reduced if the Applicant
provides a transit study that provides information showing that the reduction
is warranted.
